Perfect! Now I have all the information I need. Let me create a comprehensive report:

## Background LLM Generation Exploration Report

Based on my thorough analysis of the codebase, here's what I found about background LLM generation patterns:

### 1. **Summarize Package Architecture** (`/Users/dip/Repositories/cli/cmd/entire/cli/summarize/`)

**Main Files:**
- `/Users/dip/Repositories/cli/cmd/entire/cli/summarize/summarize.go` - Core API and transcript condensing
- `/Users/dip/Repositories/cli/cmd/entire/cli/summarize/claude.go` - ClaudeGenerator implementation

**Key Components:**

**`GenerateFromTranscript` Function Signature:**
```go
func GenerateFromTranscript(
    ctx context.Context,
    transcriptBytes []byte,
    filesTouched []string,
    agentType agent.AgentType,
    generator Generator  // nil-safe: defaults to ClaudeGenerator if nil
) (*checkpoint.Summary, error)
```

**Generator Interface:**
```go
type Generator interface {
    Generate(ctx context.Context, input Input) (*checkpoint.Summary, error)
}
```

This enables **pluggable LLM backends** - currently only `ClaudeGenerator` is implemented, but the interface allows for alternative generators (OpenAI, etc.) without changing the core logic.

---

### 2. **How Summarization is Invoked**

**Two Entry Points:**

1. **Auto-summarization during condensation** (`/Users/dip/Repositories/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_condensation.go:196-211`):
   - Called in `CondenseSession()` when a user commits code
   - Triggered only if `settings.IsSummarizeEnabled()` returns true
   - Non-blocking: failures are logged as warnings, condensation continues
   - Example:
     ```go
     if settings.IsSummarizeEnabled() && len(sessionData.Transcript) > 0 {
         summary, err := summarize.GenerateFromTranscript(
             summarizeCtx, scopedTranscript, sessionData.FilesTouched, 
             state.AgentType, nil  // nil uses default ClaudeGenerator
         )
         if err != nil {
             logging.Warn(summarizeCtx, "summary generation failed", ...)
             // Continue without summary - non-blocking
         }
     }
     ```

2. **Manual generation via `entire explain --generate`** (`/Users/dip/Repositories/cli/cmd/entire/cli/explain.go:323`):
   - User manually requests summary for an existing checkpoint
   - Same function call, persisted via `store.UpdateSummary()`

---

### 3. **Settings Control** (`/Users/dip/Repositories/cli/cmd/entire/cli/settings/settings.go`)

**Settings Structure:**
```go
type EntireSettings struct {
    StrategyOptions map[string]any  // Contains nested options
}
```

**Configuration Path:**
```json
{
  "strategy_options": {
    "summarize": {
      "enabled": true  // or false to disable
    }
  }
}
```

**Check Functions:**
- `settings.IsSummarizeEnabled()` - package-level convenience function
- `(*EntireSettings).IsSummarizeEnabled()` - instance method
- Both return `false` by default (safe fail)

---

### 4. **LLM Client & API Integration**

**ClaudeGenerator Implementation** (`/Users/dip/Repositories/cli/cmd/entire/cli/summarize/claude.go`):

**No Direct LLM SDK** - Uses subprocess invocation instead:
```go
type ClaudeGenerator struct {
    ClaudePath string                                                    // Path to "claude" CLI
    Model      string                                                     // Model name (default: "sonnet")
    CommandRunner func(ctx context.Context, name string, args ...string) *exec.Cmd // Testable
}
```

**Key Implementation Details:**
- **Calls the `claude` CLI binary** (the Claude Code IDE extension) as a subprocess
- **Command invocation:**
  ```go
  cmd := runner(ctx, claudePath, 
      "--print", 
      "--output-format", "json", 
      "--model", model,
      "--setting-sources", ""  // Disables ALL settings to avoid interference
  )
  ```
- **Default Model:** `"sonnet"` (Sonnet 3.5 - good cost/quality balance, 1M context window)
- **Input:** Prompt passed via stdin
- **Output:** JSON response parsed from stdout

**API Keys:** Not managed by this package
- Relies on user having `claude` CLI installed and authenticated
- Authentication inherited from user's Claude Code IDE configuration
- Test error: `"claude CLI not found"` if binary isn't in PATH

**Subprocess Isolation (Security):**
```go
cmd.Dir = os.TempDir()                     // Work in /tmp to avoid repo pollution
cmd.Env = stripGitEnv(os.Environ())        // Remove all GIT_* vars
// Prevents Claude Code's internal git operations from interfering
// Prevents recursive triggering of Entire's git hooks
```

---

### 5. **No Background/Async Pattern in Summarization**

**Current Implementation is Synchronous:**
- `GenerateFromTranscript` is fully synchronous, blocks until Claude responds
- Runs **inline during condensation** (when user commits)
- Non-blocking failure handling (warnings logged, session continues)
- Condensation itself happens **synchronously in the commit hook**

**Async Patterns Elsewhere in Codebase:**
- Version check uses `context.WithTimeout` (`/Users/dip/Repositories/cli/cmd/entire/cli/versioncheck/versioncheck.go:164`)
- No goroutines or channels found in summarize package
- No background job queue system exists

**Why Synchronous?**
- Summaries are generated during commit hook (`post-commit`)
- Hook must complete before returning control to user
- User expects immediate feedback (commit completes)
- Transcript is available in-memory at condensation time

---

### 6. **Summary Data Structure** (`/Users/dip/Repositories/cli/cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/checkpoint.go`)

```go
type Summary struct {
    Intent    string           `json:"intent"`      // User's goal
    Outcome   string           `json:"outcome"`     // What was achieved
    Learnings LearningsSummary `json:"learnings"`   // Structured findings
    Friction  []string         `json:"friction"`    // Problems encountered
    OpenItems []string         `json:"open_items"`  // Deferred work
}

type LearningsSummary struct {
    Repo     []string       `json:"repo"`     // Codebase patterns
    Code     []CodeLearning `json:"code"`     // File-specific findings
    Workflow []string       `json:"workflow"` // Dev practices
}

type CodeLearning struct {
    Path    string `json:"path"`
    Line    int    `json:"line"`
    EndLine int    `json:"end_line,omitempty"`
    Finding string `json:"finding"`
}
```

**Prompt Template** - requests this exact JSON structure:
```
Analyze this development session transcript and generate a structured summary.
<transcript>...</transcript>
Return a JSON object with this exact structure:
{"intent": "...", "outcome": "...", "learnings": {...}, "friction": [...], "open_items": [...]}
```

---

### 7. **Transcript Condensing** (Before Summarization)

The summarize package includes **intelligent transcript filtering** to reduce token usage:

**Condensed Transcript Entries:**
- `[User]` prompts only (skips IDE context tags, skill injections)
- `[Assistant]` text responses
- `[Tool]` tool calls with minimal detail:
  - Skill → just the skill name
  - Read → just the file path
  - WebFetch → just the URL
  - Bash → just the command
  - Other tools → description or path

**Transcript Parsing by Agent:**
- Claude Code (JSONL format via `transcript.ParseFromBytes`)
- Gemini CLI (JSON format via `geminicli.ParseTranscript`)
- OpenCode (JSONL with different schema via `opencode.ParseMessages`)

**Compression Benefits:**
- Removes verbose skill documentation
- Strips IDE-injected context tags
- Skips empty content blocks
- Keeps only what's semantically meaningful

---

### 8. **Storage & Persistence**

**Summary is stored in checkpoint metadata:**
- **Path on `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch:** `<checkpoint-id[:2]>/<checkpoint-id[2:]>/`
- **Stored alongside:** transcript, prompts, context.md, token usage, attribution
- **Optional:** Summary can be nil if disabled, generation failed, or predates feature
- **Backwards Compatible:** Checksummed and versioned separately

**Retrieval:**
- Loaded when displaying checkpoint via `entire explain <id>`
- Included in rewind metadata
- Can be updated later if regenerated

---

### 9. **Error Handling Strategy**

**Non-blocking failures:**
```go
summary, err := summarize.GenerateFromTranscript(...)
if err != nil {
    logging.Warn(summarizeCtx, "summary generation failed",
        slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
        slog.String("error", err.Error()))
    // Continue without summary - non-blocking
}
```

**Common failures:**
- Claude CLI not installed
- Claude CLI authentication failed
- Timeout or network error
- Invalid JSON in response
- Transcript parsing error

**User Impact:** None - session continues, checkpoint is still valid, just without summary

---

### 10. **Testing Approach**

**Test Files:**
- `/Users/dip/Repositories/cli/cmd/entire/cli/summarize/summarize_test.go` - 60+ tests
- `/Users/dip/Repositories/cli/cmd/entire/cli/summarize/claude_test.go` - Generator tests

**Mock Pattern:**
```go
gen := &ClaudeGenerator{
    CommandRunner: func(ctx context.Context, name string, args ...string) *exec.Cmd {
        // Return a test command that produces valid JSON response
        return exec.CommandContext(ctx, "sh", "-c", "printf '...'")
    },
}
```

**Key Test Cases:**
- Multi-agent transcript parsing (Claude/Gemini/OpenCode)
- Tool detail extraction with minimal details
- Skill content injection filtering
- IDE context tag stripping
- Empty content skipping
- Generator nil-handling
- Git isolation verification

---

## Summary

**No background/async LLM generation pattern exists** because:
1. Summarization happens synchronously during commit hooks
2. Transcript is available in-memory at condensation time
3. Non-blocking error handling prevents delays
4. Claude CLI subprocess isolation prevents interference

**The LLM client is the external `claude` CLI**, not an SDK:
- User must have Claude Code IDE installed and authenticated
- No API keys in this package (inherited from IDE)
- Subprocess isolation prevents git hook recursion
- Model selection via CLI flag (default: Sonnet)

**Settings control** is simple and extensible:
- `strategy_options.summarize.enabled` boolean flag
- Checked at condensation time
- Defaults to disabled (safe fail)

The architecture **prioritizes reliability and non-interference** over speed - summaries are optional enhancements that never block core session operations.

Perfect! Now I have comprehensive understanding. Let me create a detailed summary report.

## Summary Report: Background LLM-Based Trail Title/Description Generation

Based on thorough exploration of the codebase, here are my findings across the five key areas:

---

### **1. Summarize Package API (`cmd/entire/cli/summarize/`)**

**Key Components:**

- **`summarize.go`** - Main entry points:
  - `GenerateFromTranscript(ctx, transcriptBytes, filesTouched, agentType, generator) (*checkpoint.Summary, error)` - High-level API
  - `Input` struct: `{Transcript []Entry, FilesTouched []string}`
  - `Generator` interface: `Generate(ctx context.Context, input Input) (*checkpoint.Summary, error)`
  - `Entry` struct: `{Type, Content, ToolName, ToolDetail}`

- **`claude.go`** - `ClaudeGenerator` implementation:
  - Invokes Claude CLI via `exec.CommandContext(ctx, "claude", "--print", "--output-format", "json", "--model", model)`
  - Passes transcript via stdin, captures stdout as JSON
  - Response: `{"result": "<json-string>"}`
  - Parses response into `checkpoint.Summary` struct
  - **Isolation:** Runs in `os.TempDir()`, strips all `GIT_*` env vars to prevent recursive hook triggering
  - **Model:** Defaults to `"sonnet"` (good cost/quality ratio)

- **`checkpoint.Summary` struct:**
  ```go
  type Summary struct {
    Intent     string            // What user wanted to accomplish (1-2 sentences)
    Outcome    string            // What was achieved (1-2 sentences)
    Learnings  LearningsSummary  // Categorized findings {Repo, Code, Workflow arrays}
    Friction   []string          // Problems/blockers encountered
    OpenItems  []string          // Tech debt, unfinished work
  }
  ```

**Usage Pattern:**
```go
input := summarize.Input{
  Transcript: condensedLines,
  FilesTouched: files,
}
summary, err := generator.Generate(ctx, input) // Returns *checkpoint.Summary
```

---

### **2. Goroutine & Background Patterns in Codebase**

**Very Limited Existing Use:**
- **Only 1 file** uses goroutines: `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/content_overlap_test.go` (testing only, no production goroutines)
- **No `sync.WaitGroup` usage** anywhere in strategy or cli packages
- **No channel usage** for coordination

**Mutex Usage (Read-Write Locking):**
- `paths/paths.go`: `repoRootMu sync.RWMutex` for caching repo root (prevents race on first lookup)
- `agent/registry.go`: `registryMu sync.RWMutex` for agent registry (factory pattern)

**Implication:** The codebase is **synchronous throughout**. Lifecycle events are dispatched sequentially by hooks. Background generation would be a NEW pattern.

---

### **3. `checkpoint.Summary` Struct & Reusability**

**Structure:**
- 5 fields: `Intent`, `Outcome`, `Learnings` (with 3 arrays), `Friction`, `OpenItems`
- JSON marshaled format
- Designed for full session summarization (not partial)

**Trail Metadata Needs:**
```go
type trail.Metadata struct {
  TrailID     trail.ID    // 12-hex-char ID
  Branch      string      
  Base        string
  Title       string      // User/generated title (80 char max per existing code)
  Description string      // Full description
  Status      trail.Status
  Author      string
  Assignees   []string
  Labels      []string
  CreatedAt   time.Time
  UpdatedAt   time.Time
  MergedAt    *time.Time
}
```

**Reusability Assessment:**
- ✅ **Yes, largely reusable** - Both need condensed transcript parsing
- ✅ Can share `summarize.Input` struct and transcript condensation logic
- ❌ **But** summary generation is overkill for titles - just need first line of prompt or a simple extract
- ⚠️ **Trail doesn't need full `checkpoint.Summary`** - just title + optional description

**Recommendation:** Create a **lighter generation path** (`GenerateTrailTitle` / `GenerateTrailDescription`) that:
- Takes only transcript + prompt
- Calls Claude with simpler prompt (extract 1-2 sentences for title)
- Returns just `{title, description}` (not full Summary struct)
- Reuses transcript condensation logic from `summarize` but not full LLM prompt

---

### **4. `AutoCreateTrailOnTurnStart` End-to-End Flow**

**Current Implementation** (in `lifecycle.go:768-791`):
```go
func autoCreateTrailOnTurnStart(prompt string) {
  // 1. Check if trails disabled
  if settings.IsTrailsDisabled() { return }
  
  // 2. Check if on non-main branch
  isDefault, branchName, err := IsOnDefaultBranch()
  if err || isDefault || branchName == "" { return }
  
  // 3. Open repo and get base branch
  repo, err := strategy.OpenRepository()
  baseBranch := strategy.GetDefaultBranchName(repo)
  
  // 4. Create trail with title from prompt
  AutoCreateTrail(repo, branchName, baseBranch, prompt)
}
```

**Data Available at TurnStart:**
- ✅ `event.Prompt` - User's initial prompt (full text)
- ✅ `branchName` - Current branch name
- ✅ `baseBranch` - Target branch for merge
- ✅ `event.SessionID` - Session identifier
- ❌ `filesTouched` - **NOT available yet** (only known after TurnEnd)
- ❌ Full transcript - **NOT available yet** (session just starting)

**Current Title Generation** (`trail_cmd.go:507-520`):
```go
func titleFromPrompt(prompt string) string {
  line, _, _ := strings.Cut(prompt, "\n")  // First line only
  title := strings.TrimSpace(line)
  if len(title) > 80 {
    title = title[:77] + "..."
  }
  return title
}
```

**Updating Trail After Creation:**
- ✅ **Yes, `store.Update()` exists** (in `trail/store.go:280-290`):
  ```go
  func (s *Store) Update(trailID trail.ID, updateFn func(*Metadata)) error {
    metadata, discussion, checkpoints, err := s.Read(trailID)
    if err != nil { return err }
    
    updateFn(metadata)           // Mutation function
    metadata.UpdatedAt = time.Now()
    
    return s.Write(metadata, discussion, checkpoints)  // Persist
  }
  ```
- ✅ **Can be called from hook later** to update title/description with LLM result

---

### **5. CRITICAL - Git Operations & Concurrency Safety 🔴**

**Git Operations on `entire/trails` Branch:**

The trail store uses go-git plumbing APIs:
- `repo.Storer.SetReference(newRef)` - Update ref (atomic)
- `repo.Storer.SetEncodedObject(obj)` - Write blob (atomic)
- `BuildTreeFromEntries()` - Build git tree from map
- `CreateBlobFromContent()` - Create blob

**Race Condition Risk - VERY HIGH:**

1. **Where writes happen:**
   - `autoCreateTrailOnTurnStart()` (TurnStart hook, synchronous) → calls `store.Write()`
   - `appendCheckpointToTrail()` (PostCommit hook, synchronous) → calls `store.AddCheckpoint()` → calls `store.Write()`
   - **Proposed:** Background goroutine calling `store.Update()`

2. **The Problem:**
   ```
   Timeline:
   T0: TurnStart hook → autoCreateTrailOnTurnStart() → store.Write() [creates trail, writes entire/trails commit]
   T1: Background goroutine starts
   T2: PostCommit hook → appendCheckpointToTrail() → store.Read() [reads old tree]
   T3: Background goroutine → store.Update() → store.Read() [reads old tree]
   T4: T2 writes new commit to entire/trails
   T5: T3 writes new commit to entire/trails (based on T2's parent, LOST T2's update)
   T6: Race condition - one update lost!
   ```

3. **Why This Is Critical:**
   - `store.Write()` does **read-modify-write**:
     ```go
     ref, entries, err := s.getBranchEntries()  // Read current
     entries[path] = updated                     // Modify
     newTreeHash, err := BuildTreeFromEntries()  // Build new tree
     commitHash, err := s.createCommit(newTreeHash, ref.Hash(), msg, ...)  // Create commit
     s.repo.Storer.SetReference(newRef)         // Write atomic, but...
     ```
   - Between `getBranchEntries()` and `SetReference()`, another writer can complete
   - go-git's `SetReference()` is atomic **per operation**, but not across the full read-modify-write cycle
   - The `repo.Storer` itself has **no locks** (it's not thread-safe for mixed reads/writes)

4. **go-git Object Database Safety:**
   - ✅ `SetReference()` is atomic for single ref update
   - ✅ `SetEncodedObject()` is atomic for single blob
   - ❌ **NO mutual exclusion** between concurrent readers and writers to the same branch
   - ❌ **NO transaction semantics** across multiple git objects

5. **Current Code Pattern - Works Because Synchronous:**
   ```go
   // manual_commit_hooks.go - appendCheckpointToTrail
   appendCheckpointToTrail(repo, cpID, head.Hash(), result.Prompts)  // Sync call in PostCommit
   
   // trail_cmd.go - AutoCreateTrail  
   AutoCreateTrail(repo, branchName, baseBranch, prompt)  // Sync call in TurnStart
   ```
   These are **both synchronous**, called from git hooks sequentially.

---

### **DESIGN IMPLICATIONS FOR BACKGROUND GENERATION:**

**If you use a background goroutine:**

```go
// UNSAFE - Race condition risk:
go func() {
  time.Sleep(delay)
  store := trail.NewStore(repo)
  store.Update(trailID, func(m *Metadata) {
    m.Title = generatedTitle
    m.Description = generatedDescription
  })
}()
```

**Scenarios where this breaks:**
1. TurnStart creates trail (T0)
2. Background goroutine starts generating (T1)
3. User manually edits trail with `entire trail update` (T2) → writes to entire/trails
4. Background goroutine finishes → `store.Update()` reads pre-T2 state → writes, **loses manual edit** (T4)

5. PostCommit hook appends checkpoint while background generation is in progress
6. Both call `store.Read()` → get same parent ref
7. Both call `store.Write()` → second write loses first one

---

### **SOLUTIONS (In Order of Recommendation):**

**Option A: File-based Lock (Simplest, Most Reliable) ✅ RECOMMENDED**
```go
lockPath := filepath.Join(repoRoot, ".git", "entire-trail-lock")
lock := flock.New(lockPath)
lock.Lock()
defer lock.Unlock()

store.Update(trailID, updateFn)  // Now safe
```
- Ensures exclusive access to entire/trails branch
- Works across processes/hooks/goroutines
- Used throughout the codebase for session state files
- **Zero changes to trail store**

**Option B: Mutex in Hook Handler**
- Keep background goroutine, but add mutex to `appendCheckpointToTrail`:
  ```go
  var trailMu sync.Mutex
  
  func appendCheckpointToTrail(...) {
    trailMu.Lock()
    defer trailMu.Unlock()
    store.AddCheckpoint(...)
  }
  ```
- **Problem:** Won't protect against manual `entire trail update` or other processes
- **Advantage:** Minimal code changes

**Option C: Transaction-like Wrapper**
- Add retries with exponential backoff:
  ```go
  for attempt := 0; attempt < 3; attempt++ {
    oldMetadata, _, _, err := store.Read(trailID)
    if err != nil { return err }
    
    newMetadata := *oldMetadata
    updateFn(&newMetadata)
    
    // Check if nothing changed in between (optimistic locking)
    err = store.Update(trailID, func(m) { *m = newMetadata })
    if err == nil { return nil }
    
    time.Sleep(time.Duration(1<<uint(attempt)) * 100*time.Millisecond)
  }
  ```
- **Problem:** Still racy; doesn't actually prevent the race
- **Advantage:** Reduces likelihood of lost updates

**Option D: Defer to Existing Hook Pattern (Most Conservative)**
- Don't use background goroutine
- Add title/description generation to existing `PostCommit` or `PrePush` hook
- Synchronous, no concurrency issues
- **Trade-off:** Adds latency to commit/push operations

---

### **SUMMARY TABLE:**

| Area | Finding | Impact |
|------|---------|--------|
| **Summarize API** | Clean `Generator` interface, reusable transcript condensation, CLI isolation | Can create lighter title generator |
| **Goroutines** | None in production code (test-only) | New pattern; needs careful concurrency handling |
| **Mutex Usage** | Only for repo root caching, agent registry | Not used for git ops |
| **checkpoint.Summary** | Full struct; overkill for trail titles | Create simpler `GenerateTrailTitle` instead |
| **AutoCreateTrailOnTurnStart** | Sync, has access to prompt, can update trail later | Title can be enhanced after session |
| **Git Ops Safety** ⚠️ | **NO mutual exclusion on entire/trails branch** | **MUST use file-based lock if using background goroutine** |

---

### **RECOMMENDED PATH:**

1. **For immediate safety:** Use file-based lock (`flock`) wrapper around `store.Update()` calls
2. **Create new function:** `GenerateTrailTitleDescription(ctx, prompt, transcript, agentType)` (reuse summarize condensation)
3. **No background goroutines initially:** Call from `PostCommit` hook synchronously (safer, proven pattern)
4. **If background later needed:** Ensure file lock is held for entire duration of Read→Modify→Write

What do you recommend? Like it would be great to spawn a cheap model from the active agent in the background, non blocking to keep things updated. But I'm now wondering that this should only happen once but in the beginning there's little context available?

> AGENT

Good point about the tension between "do it once early" vs "not enough context early on." Here's how I think about it:

**At TurnStart** you have the prompt, which is usually a decent title source ("Implement user auth", "Fix checkout bug"). But sometimes it's vague ("help me with this file") or multi-paragraph.

**At first condense** (PostCommit) you have prompts + transcript + files touched - much richer. And it only runs once per trail if we flag it.

My recommendation: **generate at the first condensation**, because:

1. **Context is much richer** - you have the actual work done, not just the intent. The title "Add checkpoints.json to trails" is better than "implement the following plan" (which might be the raw prompt).
2. **Only runs once** - we can add a `generated` boolean to trail metadata. Skip if already generated.
3. **No new concurrency** - piggybacks on existing PostCommit flow where summarization already runs.
4. **Cheap model** - use haiku via the existing Claude CLI subprocess, tiny prompt, ~1s.
5. **Graceful fallback** - trail already has the `titleFromPrompt()` title from TurnStart. LLM just upgrades it.
6. **Gated on summarize setting** - users who disabled summarization don't get surprise LLM calls.

The `titleFromPrompt()` we just added serves as the "good enough immediately" title, and the LLM enhances it once real work happens.

   104→	// Claude Code performs internal git operations (plugin cache, context gathering)

   106→	// We must both change the working directory AND strip GIT_* env vars, because

   108→	// This also prevents recursive triggering of Entire's own git hooks.
   109→	cmd.Dir = os.TempDir()

   141→
   142→	// The result field contains the actual JSON summary
   143→	resultJSON := cliResponse.Result
   144→
   145→	// Try to extract JSON if it's wrapped in markdown code blocks
   146→	resultJSON = extractJSONFromMarkdown(resultJSON)
   147→
   148→	// Parse the summary from the result
   149→	var summary checkpoint.Summary
   150→	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(resultJSON), &summary); err != nil {
   151→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse summary JSON: %w (response: %s)", err, resultJSON)
   152→	}
   153→
   154→	return &summary, nil
   155→}
   156→
   157→// buildSummarizationPrompt creates the prompt for the Claude CLI.
   158→func buildSummarizationPrompt(transcriptText string) string {
   159→	return fmt.Sprintf(summarizationPromptTemplate, transcriptText)
   160→}
   161→
   162→// stripGitEnv returns a copy of env with all GIT_* variables removed.
   163→// This prevents a subprocess from discovering or modifying the parent's git repo.
   164→func stripGitEnv(env []string) []string {
   165→	filtered := make([]string, 0, len(env))
   166→	for _, e := range env {
   167→		if !strings.HasPrefix(e, "GIT_") {
   168→			filtered = append(filtered, e)
   169→		}
   170→	}
   171→	return filtered
   172→}
   173→
   174→// extractJSONFromMarkdown attempts to extract JSON from markdown code blocks.
   175→// If the input is not wrapped in code blocks, it returns the input unchanged.
   176→func extractJSONFromMarkdown(s string) string {
   177→	s = strings.TrimSpace(s)
   178→
   179→	// Check for ```json ... ``` blocks
   180→	if strings.HasPrefix(s, "```json") {
   181→		s = strings.TrimPrefix(s, "```json")
   182→		if idx := strings.LastIndex(s, "```"); idx != -1 {
   183→			s = s[:idx]
   184→		}
   185→		return strings.TrimSpace(s)
   186→	}
   187→
   188→	// Check for ``` ... ``` blocks
   189→	if strings.HasPrefix(s, "```") {
   190→		s = strings.TrimPrefix(s, "```")
   191→		if idx := strings.LastIndex(s, "```"); idx != -1 {
   192→			s = s[:idx]
   193→		}
   194→		return strings.TrimSpace(s)
   195→	}
   196→
   197→	return s
   198→}
   199→

     1→package summarize
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"bytes"
     5→	"context"
     6→	"encoding/json"
     7→	"errors"
     8→	"fmt"
     9→	"os"
    10→	"os/exec"
    11→	"strings"
    12→
    13→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint"
    14→)
    15→
    16→// summarizationPromptTemplate is the prompt used to generate summaries via the Claude CLI.
    17→//
    18→// Security note: The transcript is wrapped in <transcript> tags to provide clear boundary
    19→// markers. This helps contain any potentially malicious content within the transcript
    20→// (e.g., prompt injection attempts in user messages or file contents) by giving the LLM
    21→// a clear structural signal about where the untrusted content begins and ends.
    22→const summarizationPromptTemplate = `Analyze this development session transcript and generate a structured summary.
    23→
    24→<transcript>
    25→%s
    26→</transcript>
    27→
    28→Return a JSON object with this exact structure:
    29→{
    30→  "intent": "What the user was trying to accomplish (1-2 sentences)",
    31→  "outcome": "What was actually achieved (1-2 sentences)",
    32→  "learnings": {
    33→    "repo": ["Codebase-specific patterns, conventions, or gotchas discovered"],
    34→    "code": [{"path": "file/path.go", "line": 42, "end_line": 56, "finding": "What was learned"}],
    35→    "workflow": ["General development practices or tool usage insights"]
    36→  },
    37→  "friction": ["Problems, blockers, or annoyances encountered"],
    38→  "open_items": ["Tech debt, unfinished work, or things to revisit later"]
    39→}
    40→
    41→Guidelines:
    42→- Be concise but specific
    43→- Include line numbers for code learnings when the transcript references specific lines
    44→- Friction should capture both blockers and minor annoyances
    45→- Open items are things intentionally deferred, not failures
    46→- Empty arrays are fine if a category doesn't apply
    47→- Return ONLY the JSON object, no markdown formatting or explanation`
    48→
    49→// DefaultModel is the default model used for summarization.
    50→// Sonnet provides a good balance of quality and cost, with 1M context window
    51→// to handle long transcripts without truncation.
    52→const DefaultModel = "sonnet"
    53→
    54→// ClaudeGenerator generates summaries using the Claude CLI.
    55→type ClaudeGenerator struct {
    56→	// ClaudePath is the path to the claude CLI executable.
    57→	// If empty, defaults to "claude" (expects it to be in PATH).
    58→	ClaudePath string
    59→
    60→	// Model is the Claude model to use for summarization.
    61→	// If empty, defaults to DefaultModel ("sonnet").
    62→	Model string
    63→
    64→	// CommandRunner allows injection of the command execution for testing.
    65→	// If nil, uses exec.CommandContext directly.
    66→	CommandRunner func(ctx context.Context, name string, args ...string) *exec.Cmd
    67→}
    68→
    69→// claudeCLIResponse represents the JSON response from the Claude CLI.
    70→type claudeCLIResponse struct {
    71→	Result string `json:"result"`
    72→}
    73→
    74→// Generate creates a summary from checkpoint data by calling the Claude CLI.
    75→func (g *ClaudeGenerator) Generate(ctx context.Context, input Input) (*checkpoint.Summary, error) {
    76→	// Format the transcript for the prompt
    77→	transcriptText := FormatCondensedTranscript(input)
    78→
    79→	// Build the prompt
    80→	prompt := buildSummarizationPrompt(transcriptText)
    81→
    82→	// Execute the Claude CLI
    83→	runner := g.CommandRunner
    84→	if runner == nil {
    85→		runner = exec.CommandContext
    86→	}
    87→
    88→	claudePath := g.ClaudePath
    89→	if claudePath == "" {
    90→		claudePath = "claude"
    91→	}
    92→
    93→	model := g.Model
    94→	if model == "" {
    95→		model = DefaultModel
    96→	}
    97→
    98→	// Use empty --setting-sources to skip all settings (user, project, local).
    99→	// This avoids loading MCP servers, hooks, or other config that could interfere
   100→	// with a simple --print summarization call.
   101→	cmd := runner(ctx, claudePath, "--print", "--output-format", "json", "--model", model, "--setting-sources", "")
   102→
   103→	// Fully isolate the subprocess from the user's git repo (ENT-242).
   104→	// Claude Code performs internal git operations (plugin cache, context gathering)
   105→	// that pollute the worktree index with phantom entries from its plugin cache.
   106→	// We must both change the working directory AND strip GIT_* env vars, because
   107→	// git hooks set GIT_DIR which lets Claude Code find the repo regardless of cwd.
   108→	// This also prevents recursive triggering of Entire's own git hooks.
   109→	cmd.Dir = os.TempDir()
   110→	cmd.Env = stripGitEnv(os.Environ())
   111→
   112→	// Pass prompt via stdin
   113→	cmd.Stdin = strings.NewReader(prompt)
   114→
   115→	var stdout, stderr bytes.Buffer
   116→	cmd.Stdout = &stdout
   117→	cmd.Stderr = &stderr
   118→
   119→	err := cmd.Run()
   120→	if err != nil {
   121→		// Check if the command was not found
   122→		var execErr *exec.Error
   123→		if errors.As(err, &execErr) {
   124→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("claude CLI not found: %w", err)
   125→		}
   126→
   127→		// Check for exit error
   128→		var exitErr *exec.ExitError
   129→		if errors.As(err, &exitErr) {
   130→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("claude CLI failed (exit %d): %s", exitErr.ExitCode(), stderr.String())
   131→		}
   132→
   133→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to run claude CLI: %w", err)
   134→	}
   135→
   136→	// Parse the CLI response
   137→	var cliResponse claudeCLIResponse
   138→	if err := json.Unmarshal(stdout.Bytes(), &cliResponse); err != nil {
   139→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse claude CLI response: %w", err)
   140→	}
   141→
   142→	// The result field contains the actual JSON summary
   143→	resultJSON := cliResponse.Result
   144→
   145→	// Try to extract JSON if it's wrapped in markdown code blocks
   146→	resultJSON = extractJSONFromMarkdown(resultJSON)
   147→
   148→	// Parse the summary from the result
   149→	var summary checkpoint.Summary
   150→	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(resultJSON), &summary); err != nil {
   151→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse summary JSON: %w (response: %s)", err, resultJSON)
   152→	}
   153→
   154→	return &summary, nil
   155→}
   156→
   157→// buildSummarizationPrompt creates the prompt for the Claude CLI.
   158→func buildSummarizationPrompt(transcriptText string) string {
   159→	return fmt.Sprintf(summarizationPromptTemplate, transcriptText)
   160→}
   161→
   162→// stripGitEnv returns a copy of env with all GIT_* variables removed.
   163→// This prevents a subprocess from discovering or modifying the parent's git repo.
   164→func stripGitEnv(env []string) []string {
   165→	filtered := make([]string, 0, len(env))
   166→	for _, e := range env {
   167→		if !strings.HasPrefix(e, "GIT_") {
   168→			filtered = append(filtered, e)
   169→		}
   170→	}
   171→	return filtered
   172→}
   173→
   174→// extractJSONFromMarkdown attempts to extract JSON from markdown code blocks.
   175→// If the input is not wrapped in code blocks, it returns the input unchanged.
   176→func extractJSONFromMarkdown(s string) string {
   177→	s = strings.TrimSpace(s)
   178→
   179→	// Check for ```json ... ``` blocks
   180→	if strings.HasPrefix(s, "```json") {
   181→		s = strings.TrimPrefix(s, "```json")
   182→		if idx := strings.LastIndex(s, "```"); idx != -1 {
   183→			s = s[:idx]
   184→		}
   185→		return strings.TrimSpace(s)
   186→	}
   187→
   188→	// Check for ``` ... ``` blocks
   189→	if strings.HasPrefix(s, "```") {
   190→		s = strings.TrimPrefix(s, "```")
   191→		if idx := strings.LastIndex(s, "```"); idx != -1 {
   192→			s = s[:idx]
   193→		}
   194→		return strings.TrimSpace(s)
   195→	}
   196→
   197→	return s
   198→}
   199→

     1→// Package summarize provides AI-powered summarization of development sessions.
     2→package summarize
     3→
     4→import (
     5→	"context"
     6→	"encoding/json"
     7→	"errors"
     8→	"fmt"
     9→	"strings"
    10→
    11→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent"
    12→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent/geminicli"
    13→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/agent/opencode"
    14→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint"
    15→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/transcript"
    16→)
    17→
    18→// GenerateFromTranscript generates a summary from raw transcript bytes.
    19→// This is the shared implementation used by both explain --generate and auto-summarize.
    20→//
    21→// Parameters:
    22→//   - ctx: context for cancellation
    23→//   - transcriptBytes: raw transcript bytes (JSONL or JSON format depending on agent)
    24→//   - filesTouched: list of files modified during the session
    25→//   - agentType: the agent type to determine transcript format
    26→//   - generator: summary generator to use (if nil, uses default ClaudeGenerator)
    27→//
    28→// Returns nil, error if transcript is empty or cannot be parsed.
    29→func GenerateFromTranscript(ctx context.Context, transcriptBytes []byte, filesTouched []string, agentType agent.AgentType, generator Generator) (*checkpoint.Summary, error) {
    30→	if len(transcriptBytes) == 0 {
    31→		return nil, errors.New("empty transcript")
    32→	}
    33→
    34→	// Build condensed transcript for summarization
    35→	condensed, err := BuildCondensedTranscriptFromBytes(transcriptBytes, agentType)
    36→	if err != nil {
    37→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse transcript: %w", err)
    38→	}
    39→	if len(condensed) == 0 {
    40→		return nil, errors.New("transcript has no content to summarize")
    41→	}
    42→
    43→	input := Input{
    44→		Transcript:   condensed,
    45→		FilesTouched: filesTouched,
    46→	}
    47→
    48→	// Use default generator if none provided
    49→	if generator == nil {
    50→		generator = &ClaudeGenerator{}
    51→	}
    52→
    53→	summary, err := generator.Generate(ctx, input)
    54→	if err != nil {
    55→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to generate summary: %w", err)
    56→	}
    57→
    58→	return summary, nil
    59→}
    60→
    61→// Generator generates checkpoint summaries using an LLM.
    62→type Generator interface {
    63→	// Generate creates a summary from checkpoint data.
    64→	// Returns the generated summary or an error if generation fails.
    65→	Generate(ctx context.Context, input Input) (*checkpoint.Summary, error)
    66→}
    67→
    68→// Input contains condensed checkpoint data for summarization.
    69→type Input struct {
    70→	// Transcript is the condensed transcript entries
    71→	Transcript []Entry
    72→
    73→	// FilesTouched are the files modified during the session
    74→	FilesTouched []string
    75→}
    76→
    77→// EntryType represents the type of a transcript entry.
    78→type EntryType string
    79→
    80→const (
    81→	// EntryTypeUser indicates a user prompt entry.
    82→	EntryTypeUser EntryType = "user"
    83→	// EntryTypeAssistant indicates an assistant response entry.
    84→	EntryTypeAssistant EntryType = "assistant"
    85→	// EntryTypeTool indicates a tool call entry.
    86→	EntryTypeTool EntryType = "tool"
    87→)
    88→
    89→// Entry represents one item in the condensed transcript.
    90→type Entry struct {
    91→	// Type is the entry type (user, assistant, tool)
    92→	Type EntryType
    93→
    94→	// Content is the text content for user/assistant entries
    95→	Content string
    96→
    97→	// ToolName is the name of the tool (for tool entries)
    98→	ToolName string
    99→
   100→	// ToolDetail is a description or file path (for tool entries)
   101→	ToolDetail string
   102→}
   103→
   104→// minimalDetailTools lists tools that should show only essential details in summaries.
   105→// These tools often have verbose outputs that don't add value to summarization.
   106→// The detail shown is typically just a path, URL, or identifier rather than full content.
   107→var minimalDetailTools = map[string]bool{
   108→	"Skill":    true, // Show skill name only, not loaded content
   109→	"Read":     true, // Show file path only, not file contents
   110→	"WebFetch": true, // Show URL only, not fetched content
   111→}
   112→
   113→// BuildCondensedTranscriptFromBytes parses transcript bytes and extracts a condensed view.
   114→// This is a convenience function that combines parsing and condensing.
   115→// The agentType parameter determines which parser to use (Claude/OpenCode JSONL vs Gemini JSON).
   116→func BuildCondensedTranscriptFromBytes(content []byte, agentType agent.AgentType) ([]Entry, error) {
   117→	switch agentType {
   118→	case agent.AgentTypeGemini:
   119→		return buildCondensedTranscriptFromGemini(content)
   120→	case agent.AgentTypeOpenCode:
   121→		return buildCondensedTranscriptFromOpenCode(content)
   122→	case agent.AgentTypeClaudeCode, agent.AgentTypeUnknown:
   123→		// Claude format - fall through to shared logic below
   124→	}
   125→	// Claude format (JSONL) - handles Claude Code, Unknown, and any future agent types
   126→	lines, err := transcript.ParseFromBytes(content)
   127→	if err != nil {
   128→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse transcript: %w", err)
   129→	}
   130→	return BuildCondensedTranscript(lines), nil
   131→}
   132→
   133→// buildCondensedTranscriptFromGemini parses Gemini JSON transcript and extracts a condensed view.
   134→func buildCondensedTranscriptFromGemini(content []byte) ([]Entry, error) {
   135→	geminiTranscript, err := geminicli.ParseTranscript(content)
   136→	if err != nil {
   137→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse Gemini transcript: %w", err)
   138→	}
   139→
   140→	var entries []Entry
   141→	for _, msg := range geminiTranscript.Messages {
   142→		switch msg.Type {
   143→		case geminicli.MessageTypeUser:
   144→			if msg.Content != "" {
   145→				entries = append(entries, Entry{
   146→					Type:    EntryTypeUser,
   147→					Content: msg.Content,
   148→				})
   149→			}
   150→		case geminicli.MessageTypeGemini:
   151→			// Add assistant content
   152→			if msg.Content != "" {
   153→				entries = append(entries, Entry{
   154→					Type:    EntryTypeAssistant,
   155→					Content: msg.Content,
   156→				})
   157→			}
   158→			// Add tool calls
   159→			for _, tc := range msg.ToolCalls {
   160→				entries = append(entries, Entry{
   161→					Type:       EntryTypeTool,
   162→					ToolName:   tc.Name,
   163→					ToolDetail: extractGenericToolDetail(tc.Args),
   164→				})
   165→			}
   166→		}
   167→	}
   168→
   169→	return entries, nil
   170→}
   171→
   172→// buildCondensedTranscriptFromOpenCode parses OpenCode JSONL transcript and extracts a condensed view.
   173→func buildCondensedTranscriptFromOpenCode(content []byte) ([]Entry, error) {
   174→	messages, err := opencode.ParseMessages(content)
   175→	if err != nil {
   176→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse OpenCode transcript: %w", err)
   177→	}
   178→
   179→	var entries []Entry
   180→	for _, msg := range messages {
   181→		switch msg.Role {
   182→		case "user":
   183→			if msg.Content != "" {
   184→				entries = append(entries, Entry{
   185→					Type:    EntryTypeUser,
   186→					Content: msg.Content,
   187→				})
   188→			}
   189→		case "assistant":
   190→			if msg.Content != "" {
   191→				entries = append(entries, Entry{
   192→					Type:    EntryTypeAssistant,
   193→					Content: msg.Content,
   194→				})
   195→			}
   196→			for _, part := range msg.Parts {
   197→				if part.Type == "tool" && part.State != nil {
   198→					entries = append(entries, Entry{
   199→						Type:       EntryTypeTool,
   200→						ToolName:   part.Tool,
   201→						ToolDetail: extractGenericToolDetail(part.State.Input),
   202→					})
   203→				}
   204→			}
   205→		}
   206→	}
   207→
   208→	return entries, nil
   209→}
   210→
   211→// extractGenericToolDetail extracts an appropriate detail string from a tool's input/args map.
   212→// Checks common fields in order of preference. Used by both OpenCode and Gemini condensation.
   213→func extractGenericToolDetail(input map[string]interface{}) string {
   214→	for _, key := range []string{"description", "command", "file_path", "path", "pattern"} {
   215→		if v, ok := input[key].(string); ok && v != "" {
   216→			return v
   217→		}
   218→	}
   219→	return ""
   220→}
   221→
   222→// BuildCondensedTranscript extracts a condensed view of the transcript.
   223→// It processes user prompts, assistant responses, and tool calls into
   224→// a simplified format suitable for LLM summarization.
   225→func BuildCondensedTranscript(lines []transcript.Line) []Entry {
   226→	var entries []Entry
   227→
   228→	for _, line := range lines {
   229→		switch line.Type {
   230→		case transcript.TypeUser:
   231→			if entry := extractUserEntry(line); entry != nil {
   232→				entries = append(entries, *entry)
   233→			}
   234→		case transcript.TypeAssistant:
   235→			assistantEntries := extractAssistantEntries(line)
   236→			entries = append(entries, assistantEntries...)
   237→		}
   238→	}
   239→
   240→	return entries
   241→}
   242→
   243→// skillContentPrefix identifies user messages that are skill content injections.
   244→// These are injected after a Skill tool call and contain the full skill instructions.
   245→const skillContentPrefix = "Base directory for this skill:"
   246→
   247→// extractUserEntry extracts a user entry from a transcript line.
   248→// Returns nil if the line doesn't contain a valid user prompt or is skill content.
   249→func extractUserEntry(line transcript.Line) *Entry {
   250→	content := transcript.ExtractUserContent(line.Message)
   251→	if content == "" {
   252→		return nil
   253→	}
   254→
   255→	// Skip skill content injections - these are verbose skill instructions
   256→	// injected as user messages after Skill tool invocations in Claude Code.
   257→	// The prefix "Base directory for this skill:" is added by the superpowers
   258→	// plugin when loading skill content. This filtering reduces transcript noise
   259→	// since skill content is documentation, not user intent.
   260→	if strings.HasPrefix(content, skillContentPrefix) {
   261→		return nil
   262→	}
   263→
   264→	return &Entry{
   265→		Type:    EntryTypeUser,
   266→		Content: content,
   267→	}
   268→}
   269→
   270→// extractAssistantEntries extracts assistant and tool entries from a transcript line.
   271→func extractAssistantEntries(line transcript.Line) []Entry {
   272→	var msg transcript.AssistantMessage
   273→	if err := json.Unmarshal(line.Message, &msg); err != nil {
   274→		return nil
   275→	}
   276→
   277→	var entries []Entry
   278→
   279→	for _, block := range msg.Content {
   280→		switch block.Type {
   281→		case transcript.ContentTypeText:
   282→			if block.Text != "" {
   283→				entries = append(entries, Entry{
   284→					Type:    EntryTypeAssistant,
   285→					Content: block.Text,
   286→				})
   287→			}
   288→		case transcript.ContentTypeToolUse:
   289→			var input transcript.ToolInput
   290→			_ = json.Unmarshal(block.Input, &input) //nolint:errcheck // Best-effort parsing
   291→
   292→			detail := extractToolDetail(block.Name, input)
   293→
   294→			entries = append(entries, Entry{
   295→				Type:       EntryTypeTool,
   296→				ToolName:   block.Name,
   297→				ToolDetail: detail,
   298→			})
   299→		}
   300→	}
   301→
   302→	return entries
   303→}
   304→
   305→// extractToolDetail extracts an appropriate detail string for a tool call.
   306→// For tools in minimalDetailTools, only essential identifiers are shown.
   307→// For other tools, the full detail chain is used.
   308→func extractToolDetail(toolName string, input transcript.ToolInput) string {
   309→	// For minimal detail tools, extract only the essential identifier
   310→	if minimalDetailTools[toolName] {
   311→		switch toolName {
   312→		case "Skill":
   313→			return input.Skill
   314→		case "Read":
   315→			if input.FilePath != "" {
   316→				return input.FilePath
   317→			}
   318→			return input.NotebookPath
   319→		case "WebFetch":
   320→			return input.URL
   321→		}
   322→	}
   323→
   324→	// For other tools, use the full detail chain
   325→	if input.Description != "" {
   326→		return input.Description
   327→	}
   328→	if input.Command != "" {
   329→		return input.Command
   330→	}
   331→	if input.FilePath != "" {
   332→		return input.FilePath
   333→	}
   334→	if input.NotebookPath != "" {
   335→		return input.NotebookPath
   336→	}
   337→	return input.Pattern
   338→}
   339→
   340→// FormatCondensedTranscript formats an Input into a human-readable string for LLM.
   341→// The format is:
   342→//
   343→//	[User] user prompt here
   344→//
   345→//	[Assistant] assistant response here
   346→//
   347→//	[Tool] ToolName: description or file path
   348→func FormatCondensedTranscript(input Input) string {
   349→	var sb strings.Builder
   350→
   351→	for i, entry := range input.Transcript {
   352→		if i > 0 {
   353→			sb.WriteString("\n")
   354→		}
   355→
   356→		switch entry.Type {
   357→		case EntryTypeUser:
   358→			sb.WriteString("[User] ")
   359→			sb.WriteString(entry.Content)
   360→			sb.WriteString("\n")
   361→		case EntryTypeAssistant:
   362→			sb.WriteString("[Assistant] ")
   363→			sb.WriteString(entry.Content)
   364→			sb.WriteString("\n")
   365→		case EntryTypeTool:
   366→			sb.WriteString("[Tool] ")
   367→			sb.WriteString(entry.ToolName)
   368→			if entry.ToolDetail != "" {
   369→				sb.WriteString(": ")
   370→				sb.WriteString(entry.ToolDetail)
   371→			}
   372→			sb.WriteString("\n")
   373→		}
   374→	}
   375→
   376→	if len(input.FilesTouched) > 0 {
   377→		sb.WriteString("\n[Files Modified]\n")
   378→		for _, file := range input.FilesTouched {
   379→			fmt.Fprintf(&sb, "- %s\n", file)
   380→		}
   381→	}
   382→
   383→	return sb.String()
   384→}
   385→

    33→// hasTTY checks if /dev/tty is available for interactive prompts.
    34→// Returns false when running as an agent subprocess (no controlling terminal).
    35→//
    36→// In test environments, ENTIRE_TEST_TTY overrides the real check:
    37→//   - ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1 → simulate human (TTY available)
    38→//   - ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=0 → simulate agent (no TTY)
    39→func hasTTY() bool {
    40→	if v := os.Getenv("ENTIRE_TEST_TTY"); v != "" {
    41→		return v == "1"
    42→	}
    43→
    44→	// Gemini CLI sets GEMINI_CLI=1 when running shell commands.
    45→	// Gemini subprocesses may have access to the user's TTY, but they can't
    46→	// actually respond to interactive prompts. Treat them as non-TTY.
    47→	// See: https://geminicli.com/docs/tools/shell/
    48→	if os.Getenv("GEMINI_CLI") != "" {
    49→		return false
    50→	}
    51→
    52→	tty, err := os.OpenFile("/dev/tty", os.O_RDWR, 0)
    53→	if err != nil {
    54→		return false
    55→	}
    56→	_ = tty.Close()
    57→	return true
    58→}
    59→
    60→// askConfirmTTY prompts the user for a yes/no confirmation via /dev/tty.
    61→// This works even when stdin is redirected (e.g., git commit -m).
    62→// Returns true for yes, false for no. If TTY is unavailable, returns the default.
    63→// If context is non-empty, it is displayed on a separate line before the prompt.
    64→func askConfirmTTY(prompt string, context string, defaultYes bool) bool {
    65→	// In test mode, don't try to interact with the real TTY — just use the default.
    66→	// ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=1 simulates "a human is present" for the hasTTY() check
    67→	// but we can't actually read from the TTY in tests.
    68→	if os.Getenv("ENTIRE_TEST_TTY") != "" {
    69→		return defaultYes
    70→	}
    71→
    72→	// Gemini CLI sets GEMINI_CLI=1 when running shell commands (including git commit).
    73→	// The agent can't respond to TTY prompts, so use the default to avoid hanging.
    74→	// See: https://geminicli.com/docs/tools/shell/
    75→	if os.Getenv("GEMINI_CLI") != "" {
    76→		return defaultYes
    77→	}
    78→
    79→	// Open /dev/tty for both reading and writing
    80→	// This is the controlling terminal, which works even when stdin/stderr are redirected
    81→	tty, err := os.OpenFile("/dev/tty", os.O_RDWR, 0)
    82→	if err != nil {
    83→		// Can't open TTY (e.g., running in CI), use default
    84→		return defaultYes
    85→	}
    86→	defer tty.Close()
    87→
    88→	// Show context if provided
    89→	if context != "" {
    90→		fmt.Fprintf(tty, "%s\n", context)
    91→	}
    92→
    93→	// Show prompt with default indicator
    94→	// Write to tty directly, not stderr, since git hooks may redirect stderr to /dev/null
    95→	var hint string
    96→	if defaultYes {
    97→		hint = "[Y/n]"
    98→	} else {
    99→		hint = "[y/N]"
   100→	}
   101→	fmt.Fprintf(tty, "%s %s ", prompt, hint)
   102→
   103→	// Read response
   104→	reader := bufio.NewReader(tty)
   105→	response, err := reader.ReadString('\n')
   106→	if err != nil {
   107→		return defaultYes
   108→	}
   109→
   110→	response = strings.TrimSpace(strings.ToLower(response))
   111→	switch response {
   112→	case "y", "yes":
   113→		return true
   114→	case "n", "no":
   115→		return false
   116→	default:
   117→		// Empty or invalid input - use default
   118→		return defaultYes
   119→	}
   120→}
   121→
   122→// CommitMsg is called by the git commit-msg hook after the user edits the message.
   123→// If the message contains only our trailer (no actual user content), strip it
   124→// so git will abort the commit due to empty message.
   125→//
   126→//nolint:unparam // error return required by interface but hooks must return nil
   127→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) CommitMsg(commitMsgFile string) error {
   128→	content, err := os.ReadFile(commitMsgFile) //nolint:gosec // Path comes from git hook
   129→	if err != nil {
   130→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   131→	}
   132→
   133→	message := string(content)
   134→
   135→	// Check if our trailer is present (ParseCheckpoint validates format, so found==true means valid)
   136→	if _, found := trailers.ParseCheckpoint(message); !found {
   137→		// No trailer, nothing to do
   138→		return nil
   139→	}
   140→
   141→	// Check if there's any user content (non-comment, non-trailer lines)
   142→	if !hasUserContent(message) {
   143→		// No user content - strip the trailer so git aborts
   144→		message = stripCheckpointTrailer(message)
   145→		if err := os.WriteFile(commitMsgFile, []byte(message), 0o600); err != nil {
   146→			return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   147→		}
   148→	}
   149→
   150→	return nil
   151→}
   152→
   153→// hasUserContent checks if the message has any content besides comments and our trailer.
   154→func hasUserContent(message string) bool {
   155→	trailerPrefix := trailers.CheckpointTrailerKey + ":"
   156→	for _, line := range strings.Split(message, "\n") {
   157→		trimmed := strings.TrimSpace(line)
   158→		// Skip empty lines
   159→		if trimmed == "" {
   160→			continue
   161→		}
   162→		// Skip comment lines
   163→		if strings.HasPrefix(trimmed, "#") {
   164→			continue
   165→		}
   166→		// Skip our trailer line
   167→		if strings.HasPrefix(trimmed, trailerPrefix) {
   168→			continue
   169→		}
   170→		// Found user content
   171→		return true
   172→	}
   173→	return false
   174→}
   175→
   176→// stripCheckpointTrailer removes the Entire-Checkpoint trailer line from the message.
   177→func stripCheckpointTrailer(message string) string {
   178→	trailerPrefix := trailers.CheckpointTrailerKey + ":"
   179→	var result []string
   180→	for _, line := range strings.Split(message, "\n") {
   181→		if !strings.HasPrefix(strings.TrimSpace(line), trailerPrefix) {
   182→			result = append(result, line)
   183→		}
   184→	}
   185→	return strings.Join(result, "\n")
   186→}
   187→
   188→// isGitSequenceOperation checks if git is currently in the middle of a rebase,
   189→// cherry-pick, or revert operation. During these operations, commits are being
   190→// replayed and should not be linked to agent sessions.
   191→//
   192→// Detects:
   193→//   - rebase: .git/rebase-merge/ or .git/rebase-apply/ directories
   194→//   - cherry-pick: .git/CHERRY_PICK_HEAD file
   195→//   - revert: .git/REVERT_HEAD file
   196→func isGitSequenceOperation() bool {
   197→	// Get git directory (handles worktrees and relative paths correctly)
   198→	gitDir, err := GetGitDir()
   199→	if err != nil {
   200→		return false // Can't determine, assume not in sequence operation
   201→	}
   202→
   203→	// Check for rebase state directories
   204→	if _, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(gitDir, "rebase-merge")); err == nil {
   205→		return true
   206→	}
   207→	if _, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(gitDir, "rebase-apply")); err == nil {
   208→		return true
   209→	}
   210→
   211→	// Check for cherry-pick and revert state files
   212→	if _, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(gitDir, "CHERRY_PICK_HEAD")); err == nil {
   213→		return true
   214→	}
   215→	if _, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(gitDir, "REVERT_HEAD")); err == nil {
   216→		return true
   217→	}
   218→
   219→	return false
   220→}
   221→
   222→// PrepareCommitMsg is called by the git prepare-commit-msg hook.
   223→// Adds an Entire-Checkpoint trailer to the commit message with a stable checkpoint ID.
   224→// Only adds a trailer if there's actually new session content to condense.
   225→// The actual condensation happens in PostCommit - if the user removes the trailer,
   226→// the commit will not be linked to the session (useful for "manual" commits).
   227→// For amended commits, preserves the existing checkpoint ID.
   228→//
   229→// The source parameter indicates how the commit was initiated:
   230→//   - "" or "template": normal editor flow - adds trailer with explanatory comment
   231→//   - "message": using -m or -F flag - prompts user interactively via /dev/tty
   232→//   - "merge", "squash": skip trailer entirely (auto-generated messages)
   233→//   - "commit": amend operation - preserves existing trailer or restores from LastCheckpointID
   234→//
   235→
   236→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) PrepareCommitMsg(commitMsgFile string, source string) error {
   237→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
   238→
   239→	// Skip during rebase, cherry-pick, or revert operations
   240→	// These are replaying existing commits and should not be linked to agent sessions
   241→	if isGitSequenceOperation() {
   242→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "prepare-commit-msg: skipped during git sequence operation",
   243→			slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   244→			slog.String("source", source),
   245→		)
   246→		return nil
   247→	}
   248→
   249→	// Skip for merge and squash sources
   250→	// These are auto-generated messages - not from Claude sessions
   251→	switch source {
   252→	case "merge", "squash":
   253→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "prepare-commit-msg: skipped for source",
   254→			slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   255→			slog.String("source", source),
   256→		)
   257→		return nil
   258→	}
   259→
   260→	// Handle amend (source="commit") separately: preserve or restore trailer
   261→	if source == "commit" {
   262→		return s.handleAmendCommitMsg(logCtx, commitMsgFile)
   263→	}
   264→
   265→	repo, err := OpenRepository()
   266→	if err != nil {
   267→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   268→	}
   269→
   270→	worktreePath, err := GetWorktreePath()
   271→	if err != nil {
   272→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   273→	}
   274→
   275→	// Find all active sessions for this worktree
   276→	// We match by worktree (not BaseCommit) because the user may have made
   277→	// intermediate commits without entering new prompts, causing HEAD to diverge
   278→	sessions, err := s.findSessionsForWorktree(worktreePath)
   279→	if err != nil || len(sessions) == 0 {
   280→		// No active sessions or error listing - silently skip (hooks must be resilient)
   281→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "prepare-commit-msg: no active sessions",
   282→			slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   283→			slog.String("source", source),
   284→		)
   285→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Intentional: hooks must be silent on failure
   286→	}
   287→
   288→	// Fast path: when an agent is committing (ACTIVE session + no TTY), skip
   289→	// content detection and interactive prompts. The agent can't respond to TTY
   290→	// prompts and the content detection can miss mid-session work (no shadow
   291→	// branch yet, transcript analysis may fail). Generate a checkpoint ID and
   292→	// add the trailer directly.
   293→	if !hasTTY() {
   294→		for _, state := range sessions {
   295→			if state.Phase.IsActive() {
   296→				return s.addTrailerForAgentCommit(logCtx, commitMsgFile, state, source)
   297→			}
   298→		}
   299→	}
   300→
   301→	// Check if any session has new content to condense
   302→	sessionsWithContent := s.filterSessionsWithNewContent(repo, sessions)
   303→
   304→	if len(sessionsWithContent) == 0 {
   305→		// No new content — no trailer needed
   306→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "prepare-commit-msg: no content to link",
   307→			slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   308→			slog.String("source", source),
   309→			slog.Int("sessions_found", len(sessions)),
   310→		)
   311→		return nil
   312→	}
   313→
   314→	// Read current commit message
   315→	content, err := os.ReadFile(commitMsgFile) //nolint:gosec // commitMsgFile is provided by git hook
   316→	if err != nil {
   317→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   318→	}
   319→
   320→	message := string(content)
   321→
   322→	// Check if trailer already exists (ParseCheckpoint validates format, so found==true means valid)
   323→	if existingCpID, found := trailers.ParseCheckpoint(message); found {
   324→		// Trailer already exists (e.g., amend) - keep it
   325→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "prepare-commit-msg: trailer already exists",
   326→			slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   327→			slog.String("source", source),
   328→			slog.String("existing_checkpoint_id", existingCpID.String()),
   329→		)
   330→		return nil
   331→	}
   332→
   333→	// Generate a fresh checkpoint ID
   334→	checkpointID, err := id.Generate()
   335→	if err != nil {
   336→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to generate checkpoint ID: %w", err)
   337→	}
   338→
   339→	// Determine agent type and last prompt from session
   340→	agentType := DefaultAgentType // default for backward compatibility
   341→	var lastPrompt string
   342→	if len(sessionsWithContent) > 0 {
   343→		firstSession := sessionsWithContent[0]
   344→		if firstSession.AgentType != "" {
   345→			agentType = firstSession.AgentType
   346→		}
   347→		lastPrompt = s.getLastPrompt(repo, firstSession)
   348→	}
   349→
   350→	// Prepare prompt for display: collapse newlines/whitespace, then truncate (rune-safe)
   351→	displayPrompt := stringutil.TruncateRunes(stringutil.CollapseWhitespace(lastPrompt), 80, "...")
   352→
   353→	// Add trailer differently based on commit source
   354→	switch source {
   355→	case "message":
   356→		// Using -m or -F: ask user interactively whether to add trailer
   357→		// (comments won't be stripped by git in this mode)
   358→
   359→		// Build context string for interactive prompt
   360→		var promptContext string
   361→		if displayPrompt != "" {
   362→			promptContext = "You have an active " + string(agentType) + " session.\nLast Prompt: " + displayPrompt
   363→		}
   364→
   365→		if !askConfirmTTY("Link this commit to "+string(agentType)+" session context?", promptContext, true) {
   366→			// User declined - don't add trailer
   367→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "prepare-commit-msg: user declined trailer",
   368→				slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   369→				slog.String("source", source),
   370→			)
   371→			return nil
   372→		}
   373→		message = addCheckpointTrailer(message, checkpointID)
   374→	default:
   375→		// Normal editor flow: add trailer with explanatory comment (will be stripped by git)
   376→		message = addCheckpointTrailerWithComment(message, checkpointID, string(agentType), displayPrompt)
   377→	}
   378→
   379→	logging.Info(logCtx, "prepare-commit-msg: trailer added",
   380→		slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   381→		slog.String("source", source),
   382→		slog.String("checkpoint_id", checkpointID.String()),
   383→	)
   384→
   385→	// Write updated message back
   386→	if err := os.WriteFile(commitMsgFile, []byte(message), 0o600); err != nil {
   387→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   388→	}
   389→
   390→	return nil
   391→}
   392→
   393→// handleAmendCommitMsg handles the prepare-commit-msg hook for amend operations
   394→// (source="commit"). It preserves existing trailers or restores from LastCheckpointID.
   395→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) handleAmendCommitMsg(logCtx context.Context, commitMsgFile string) error {
   396→	// Read current commit message
   397→	content, err := os.ReadFile(commitMsgFile) //nolint:gosec // commitMsgFile is provided by git hook
   398→	if err != nil {
   399→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   400→	}
   401→
   402→	message := string(content)
   403→
   404→	// If message already has a trailer, keep it unchanged
   405→	if existingCpID, found := trailers.ParseCheckpoint(message); found {
   406→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "prepare-commit-msg: amend preserves existing trailer",
   407→			slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   408→			slog.String("checkpoint_id", existingCpID.String()),
   409→		)
   410→		return nil
   411→	}
   412→
   413→	// No trailer in message — check if any session has LastCheckpointID to restore
   414→	worktreePath, err := GetWorktreePath()
   415→	if err != nil {
   416→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   417→	}
   418→
   419→	sessions, err := s.findSessionsForWorktree(worktreePath)
   420→	if err != nil || len(sessions) == 0 {
   421→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // No sessions - nothing to restore
   422→	}
   423→
   424→	// For amend, HEAD^ is the commit being amended, and HEAD is where we are now.
   425→	// We need to match sessions whose BaseCommit equals HEAD (the commit being amended
   426→	// was created from this base). This prevents stale sessions from injecting
   427→	// unrelated checkpoint IDs.
   428→	repo, repoErr := OpenRepository()
   429→	if repoErr != nil {
   430→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   431→	}
   432→	head, headErr := repo.Head()
   433→	if headErr != nil {
   434→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   435→	}
   436→	currentHead := head.Hash().String()
   437→
   438→	// Find first matching session with LastCheckpointID to restore.
   439→	// LastCheckpointID is set after condensation completes.
   440→	for _, state := range sessions {
   441→		if state.BaseCommit != currentHead {
   442→			continue
   443→		}
   444→		if state.LastCheckpointID.IsEmpty() {
   445→			continue
   446→		}
   447→		cpID := state.LastCheckpointID
   448→		source := "LastCheckpointID"
   449→
   450→		// Restore the trailer
   451→		message = addCheckpointTrailer(message, cpID)
   452→		if writeErr := os.WriteFile(commitMsgFile, []byte(message), 0o600); writeErr != nil {
   453→			return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   454→		}
   455→
   456→		logging.Info(logCtx, "prepare-commit-msg: restored trailer on amend",
   457→			slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   458→			slog.String("checkpoint_id", cpID.String()),
   459→			slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
   460→			slog.String("source", source),
   461→		)
   462→		return nil
   463→	}
   464→
   465→	// No checkpoint ID found - leave message unchanged
   466→	logging.Debug(logCtx, "prepare-commit-msg: amend with no checkpoint to restore",
   467→		slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   468→	)
   469→	return nil
   470→}
   471→
   472→// PostCommit is called by the git post-commit hook after a commit is created.
   473→// Uses the session state machine to determine what action to take per session:
   474→//   - ACTIVE → condense immediately (each commit gets its own checkpoint)
   475→//   - IDLE → condense immediately
   476→//   - ENDED → condense if files touched, discard if empty
   477→//
   478→// After condensation for ACTIVE sessions, remaining uncommitted files are
   479→// carried forward to a new shadow branch so the next commit gets its own checkpoint.
   480→//
   481→// Shadow branches are only deleted when ALL sessions sharing the branch are non-active
   482→// and were condensed during this PostCommit.
   483→
   484→// postCommitActionHandler implements session.ActionHandler for PostCommit.
   485→// Each session in the loop gets its own handler with per-session context.
   486→// Handler methods use the *State parameter from ApplyTransition (same pointer
   487→// as the state being transitioned) rather than capturing state separately.
   488→type postCommitActionHandler struct {
   489→	s                      *ManualCommitStrategy
   490→	logCtx                 context.Context
   491→	repo                   *git.Repository
   492→	checkpointID           id.CheckpointID
   493→	head                   *plumbing.Reference
   494→	commit                 *object.Commit
   495→	newHead                string
   496→	shadowBranchName       string
   497→	shadowBranchesToDelete map[string]struct{}
   498→	committedFileSet       map[string]struct{}
   499→	hasNew                 bool
   500→	filesTouchedBefore     []string
   501→
   502→	// Output: set by handler methods, read by caller after TransitionAndLog.
   503→	condensed bool
   504→}
   505→
   506→func (h *postCommitActionHandler) HandleCondense(state *session.State) error {
   507→	shouldCondense := h.shouldCondenseWithOverlapCheck(state.Phase.IsActive())
   508→
   509→	logging.Debug(h.logCtx, "post-commit: HandleCondense decision",
   510→		slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
   511→		slog.String("phase", string(state.Phase)),
   512→		slog.Bool("has_new", h.hasNew),
   513→		slog.Bool("should_condense", shouldCondense),
   514→		slog.String("shadow_branch", h.shadowBranchName),
   515→	)
   516→
   517→	if shouldCondense {
   518→		h.condensed = h.s.condenseAndUpdateState(h.logCtx, h.repo, h.checkpointID, state, h.head, h.shadowBranchName, h.shadowBranchesToDelete, h.committedFileSet)
   519→	} else {
   520→		h.s.updateBaseCommitIfChanged(h.logCtx, state, h.newHead)
   521→	}
   522→	return nil
   523→}
   524→
   525→func (h *postCommitActionHandler) HandleCondenseIfFilesTouched(state *session.State) error {
   526→	shouldCondense := len(state.FilesTouched) > 0 && h.shouldCondenseWithOverlapCheck(state.Phase.IsActive())
   527→
   528→	logging.Debug(h.logCtx, "post-commit: HandleCondenseIfFilesTouched decision",
   529→		slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
   530→		slog.String("phase", string(state.Phase)),
   531→		slog.Bool("has_new", h.hasNew),
   532→		slog.Int("files_touched", len(state.FilesTouched)),
   533→		slog.Bool("should_condense", shouldCondense),
   534→		slog.String("shadow_branch", h.shadowBranchName),
   535→	)
   536→
   537→	if shouldCondense {
   538→		h.condensed = h.s.condenseAndUpdateState(h.logCtx, h.repo, h.checkpointID, state, h.head, h.shadowBranchName, h.shadowBranchesToDelete, h.committedFileSet)
   539→	} else {
   540→		h.s.updateBaseCommitIfChanged(h.logCtx, state, h.newHead)
   541→	}
   542→	return nil
   543→}
   544→
   545→// shouldCondenseWithOverlapCheck returns true if the session should be condensed
   546→// into this commit. Requires both that hasNew is true AND that the session's files
   547→// overlap with the committed files with matching content.
   548→//
   549→// This prevents stale sessions (ACTIVE sessions where the agent was killed, or
   550→// ENDED/IDLE sessions with carry-forward files) from being condensed into every
   551→// unrelated commit.
   552→//
   553→// filesTouchedBefore is populated from:
   554→//   - state.FilesTouched for IDLE/ENDED sessions (set via SaveStep/SaveTaskStep -> mergeFilesTouched)
   555→//   - transcript extraction for ACTIVE sessions when FilesTouched is empty
   556→//
   557→// When filesTouchedBefore is empty:
   558→//   - For ACTIVE sessions: fail-open (trust hasNew) because the agent may be
   559→//     mid-turn before any files are saved to state.
   560→//   - For IDLE/ENDED sessions: return false because there are no files to
   561→//     overlap with the commit.
   562→func (h *postCommitActionHandler) shouldCondenseWithOverlapCheck(isActive bool) bool {
   563→	if !h.hasNew {
   564→		return false
   565→	}
   566→	if len(h.filesTouchedBefore) == 0 {
   567→		return isActive // ACTIVE: fail-open; IDLE/ENDED: no files = no overlap
   568→	}
   569→	// Only check files that were actually changed in this commit.
   570→	// Without this, files that exist in the tree but weren't changed
   571→	// would pass the "modified file" check in filesOverlapWithContent
   572→	// (because the file exists in the parent tree), causing stale
   573→	// sessions to be incorrectly condensed.
   574→	var committedTouchedFiles []string
   575→	for _, f := range h.filesTouchedBefore {
   576→		if _, ok := h.committedFileSet[f]; ok {
   577→			committedTouchedFiles = append(committedTouchedFiles, f)
   578→		}
   579→	}
   580→	if len(committedTouchedFiles) == 0 {
   581→		return false
   582→	}
   583→	return filesOverlapWithContent(h.repo, h.shadowBranchName, h.commit, committedTouchedFiles)
   584→}
   585→
   586→func (h *postCommitActionHandler) HandleDiscardIfNoFiles(state *session.State) error {
   587→	if len(state.FilesTouched) == 0 {
   588→		logging.Debug(h.logCtx, "post-commit: skipping empty ended session (no files to condense)",
   589→			slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
   590→		)
   591→	}
   592→	h.s.updateBaseCommitIfChanged(h.logCtx, state, h.newHead)
   593→	return nil
   594→}
   595→
   596→func (h *postCommitActionHandler) HandleWarnStaleSession(_ *session.State) error {
   597→	// Not produced by EventGitCommit; no-op for exhaustiveness.
   598→	return nil
   599→}
   600→
   601→// During rebase/cherry-pick/revert operations, phase transitions are skipped entirely.
   602→//
   603→//nolint:unparam // error return required by interface but hooks must return nil
   604→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) PostCommit() error {
   605→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
   606→
   607→	repo, err := OpenRepository()
   608→	if err != nil {
   609→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   610→	}
   611→
   612→	// Get HEAD commit to check for trailer
   613→	head, err := repo.Head()
   614→	if err != nil {
   615→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   616→	}
   617→
   618→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(head.Hash())
   619→	if err != nil {
   620→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   621→	}
   622→
   623→	// Check if commit has checkpoint trailer (ParseCheckpoint validates format)
   624→	checkpointID, found := trailers.ParseCheckpoint(commit.Message)
   625→	if !found {
   626→		// No trailer — user removed it or it was never added (mid-turn commit).
   627→		// Still update BaseCommit for active sessions so future commits can match.
   628→		s.postCommitUpdateBaseCommitOnly(logCtx, head)
   629→		return nil
   630→	}
   631→
   632→	worktreePath, err := GetWorktreePath()
   633→	if err != nil {
   634→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   635→	}
   636→
   637→	// Find all active sessions for this worktree
   638→	sessions, err := s.findSessionsForWorktree(worktreePath)
   639→	if err != nil || len(sessions) == 0 {
   640→		logging.Warn(logCtx, "post-commit: no active sessions despite trailer",
   641→			slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   642→			slog.String("checkpoint_id", checkpointID.String()),
   643→		)
   644→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Intentional: hooks must be silent on failure
   645→	}
   646→
   647→	// Build transition context
   648→	isRebase := isGitSequenceOperation()
   649→	transitionCtx := session.TransitionContext{
   650→		IsRebaseInProgress: isRebase,
   651→	}
   652→
   653→	if isRebase {
   654→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "post-commit: rebase/sequence in progress, skipping phase transitions",
   655→			slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   656→		)
   657→	}
   658→
   659→	// Track shadow branch names and whether they can be deleted
   660→	shadowBranchesToDelete := make(map[string]struct{})
   661→	// Track active sessions that were NOT condensed — their shadow branches must be preserved
   662→	uncondensedActiveOnBranch := make(map[string]bool)
   663→
   664→	newHead := head.Hash().String()
   665→	committedFileSet := filesChangedInCommit(commit)
   666→
   667→	for _, state := range sessions {
   668→		shadowBranchName := getShadowBranchNameForCommit(state.BaseCommit, state.WorktreeID)
   669→
   670→		// Check for new content (needed for TransitionContext and condensation).
   671→		// Fail-open: if content check errors, assume new content exists so we
   672→		// don't silently skip data that should have been condensed.
   673→		//
   674→		// For ACTIVE sessions: the commit has a checkpoint trailer (verified above),
   675→		// meaning PrepareCommitMsg already determined this commit is session-related.
   676→		// The trailer is only added when either:
   677→		//   - No TTY (agent/subagent committing) — added unconditionally
   678→		//   - TTY (human committing) — added after content detection confirmed agent work
   679→		// In both cases, PrepareCommitMsg already validated this commit. We trust
   680→		// that decision here. Transcript-based re-validation is unreliable because
   681→		// subagent transcripts may not be available yet (subagent still running).
   682→		var hasNew bool
   683→		if state.Phase.IsActive() {
   684→			hasNew = true
   685→		} else {
   686→			var contentErr error
   687→			hasNew, contentErr = s.sessionHasNewContent(repo, state)
   688→			if contentErr != nil {
   689→				hasNew = true
   690→				logging.Debug(logCtx, "post-commit: error checking session content, assuming new content",
   691→					slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
   692→					slog.String("error", contentErr.Error()),
   693→				)
   694→			}
   695→		}
   696→		transitionCtx.HasFilesTouched = len(state.FilesTouched) > 0
   697→
   698→		// Save FilesTouched BEFORE TransitionAndLog — the handler's condensation
   699→		// clears it, but we need the original list for carry-forward computation.
   700→		// For mid-session commits (ACTIVE, no shadow branch), state.FilesTouched may be empty
   701→		// because no SaveStep/Stop has been called yet. Extract files from transcript.
   702→		filesTouchedBefore := make([]string, len(state.FilesTouched))
   703→		copy(filesTouchedBefore, state.FilesTouched)
   704→		if len(filesTouchedBefore) == 0 && state.Phase.IsActive() && state.TranscriptPath != "" {
   705→			filesTouchedBefore = s.extractFilesFromLiveTranscript(state)
   706→		}
   707→
   708→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "post-commit: carry-forward prep",
   709→			slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
   710→			slog.Bool("is_active", state.Phase.IsActive()),
   711→			slog.String("transcript_path", state.TranscriptPath),
   712→			slog.Int("files_touched_before", len(filesTouchedBefore)),
   713→			slog.Any("files", filesTouchedBefore),
   714→		)
   715→
   716→		// Run the state machine transition with handler for strategy-specific actions.
   717→		handler := &postCommitActionHandler{
   718→			s:                      s,
   719→			logCtx:                 logCtx,
   720→			repo:                   repo,
   721→			checkpointID:           checkpointID,
   722→			head:                   head,
   723→			commit:                 commit,
   724→			newHead:                newHead,
   725→			shadowBranchName:       shadowBranchName,
   726→			shadowBranchesToDelete: shadowBranchesToDelete,
   727→			committedFileSet:       committedFileSet,
   728→			hasNew:                 hasNew,
   729→			filesTouchedBefore:     filesTouchedBefore,
   730→		}
   731→
   732→		if err := TransitionAndLog(state, session.EventGitCommit, transitionCtx, handler); err != nil {
   733→			fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "[entire] Warning: post-commit action handler error: %v\n", err)
   734→		}
   735→
